股價相對大盤走勢

台玻(1802 TT) Buy

破曉巨浪已然凝聚。 
投資建議：高階玻纖布 Low Dk2/Low CTE出貨動能攀升，帶動
3Q26 毛利率季增逾 8%， 10/2026 營收創高。研究部同步上修
4Q26、 2027 年獲利預估。預估台玻 2027 年稅後 EPS 4.84 元。
給予投資建議 Buy，目標價 100 元 (2027 年 PBR3.97X)。  
3Q26 毛利率再創高至 31.60%：台玻 3Q26 受惠於 Low Dk2 、
Low CTE 出貨比重拉升，3Q26 毛利率較 2Q26 提升 8.27%至
31.60%，亦優於原預期之 26.23%。 3Q26 稅後純益 27.05 億元、
稅後 EPS 0.93 元，均優於預期。 
10/2026 營收 MoM+12.04%、 YoY+56.49%：台玻 10/2026 營收
55.66 億元，MoM+12.04%、YoY+56.49%，優於預期，主因玻纖
布事業營收持續攀升，整體出貨動能較 09/2026 成長。以研究
部預期當前玻纖布營收比重約 40~46%，高階玻纖布占其中
40~42%推估，10/2026 高階玻纖布營收貢獻 MoM+13~16%。  
預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 比重仍高於 Low CTE：台玻高階玻纖布
以 Low Dk2/Low CTE 為主要營收來源。Low CTE 已於 2025 年
底開始小量出貨，目前產品出貨量已較 2026 年初顯著增加，預
期營收佔比將於 2027 年持續提升，並於 2H27~2028 年新產能
開出後，追趕 Low Dk2 。預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 營收比重仍將
高於 Low Dk1 及 Low CTE。  
積極擴建 Low Dk/Low CTE產能，期待擴產效益顯現，預估 2027
年稅後 EPS 4.84 元：11/02/2026 董事會通過桃園廠、鹿港廠窯
爐增建及子公司台嘉蚌埠玻璃纖維有限公司冷修擴充案，預估
投入金額約新台幣 16.3 億元及人民幣 11.5 億元。Low CTE 產
品已取得國內載板大廠認證，2028 年訂單洽談順暢。研究部預
估台玻 2027 年營收 711.51 億元，YoY+33.24%，稅後純益 140.68
億元，YoY+69.11%，稅後 EPS 4.84 元。

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

2 群益投顧 
  台玻 ESG： 
ESG 方面，台玻堅守四大主軸，包括強化永續環境、落實誠信經營、關懷社
會公益及發展友善職場。強化永續環境方面，台玻在符合法規條件下，積極
減降生產過程對環境的外部成本，並妥善安置生產後產生之廢棄物；落實誠
信經營方面，台玻始終秉持該核心經營理念，與對股東、員工及利害關係人
之承諾及責任；關懷社會公益方面，透過參與社會公益活動，落實回饋社會
及關懷責任，並提供員工及經銷商子女相關獎學金，培育人才；發展友善職
場方面，尊重人權及性別平等，提供員工舒適且安全職場環境。 
公司簡介： 
台玻成立於 1964 年，事業群分別平板事業部、纖維事業部、容器事業部、
汽車玻璃事業部。主要產品包括平板玻璃、玻璃纖維及玻璃器皿，生產基地
位於台灣及中國兩地。平板玻璃產品均已通過歐盟 CE、美國 ANSI、台灣
CNS 相關規範，低輻射 Low-E 複層玻能維持可見光情況下，阻擋紅外線熱
能進入室內，具良好隔熱效果，主要用於建築物玻璃帷幕牆。公司也有發展
玻璃基板，有望搭上 CoPoS 題材。玻璃纖維方面，台玻有複合材料用的 FRP
及玻璃纖維布。FRP 玻璃纖維具備優異耐腐蝕耐候性、低成本及高強度特
性。玻璃纖維布廣泛運用於 5G 伺服器、AI 伺服器、手機平板及筆電等產
品。 
圖一、台玻產品組合(營收比重)

資料來源：台玻，群益投顧預估彙整 
台玻 2026 年高階玻纖布市占率上看 45%， 2028 年訂單洽談順暢： 
台玻從 1990 年開始生產玻纖布，技術並非一蹴可磯，而是多年人才培育及
技術經驗累積推敲而得。最開始是與美國 Owens Corning 簽署技術合作授
權，從中取得全球最先進玻璃纖維漿料配方及技術指導，並向日本 Kanebo
購入織布技術。台玻亦為繼 Nittobo、 Ibiden 後，第三家廠商具備自行研發
量產 Low Dk 玻璃纖維布廠商，並已通過國內外大廠認證，於 2025 年陸續
出貨。主要應用於 5G 基礎建設、高階伺服器、高階顯卡及車用等領域。 
AI 投資強勁推升載板加速升級，Low Dk1、 Low Dk2 玻纖布及 Low CTE 玻
纖布需求隨之暴漲。現階段台玻產能已達滿載，且訂單能見度至 2027 年，
2028 年訂單洽談順暢。公司表示，2026 年目標高階玻纖布市占率 40%~45%。

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

3 群益投顧 
台玻客戶需求達 2028 年，Nittobo 指出 2029 年度產能擴增至 2025 年度之
3.6 倍： 
台玻 T-glass 已通過國內外廠商認證，Ibiden 亦表示 T-glass 仍處於供不應
求階段。T-glass 尚待日本大廠 Nittobo 於 2H27 後開出新產能，才得以部分
緩解，且考量技術門檻偏高、窯爐建置期長，產能放量亦需時間等待。台玻
董事長更於近日指出，客戶需求已達 2028 年，當前公司供給仍追不上需求。
Nittobo 亦再次表示 T-glass 需求強勁。Nittobo 目前產線均處滿載生產階段，
且決定於台灣工廠增建玻纖絲製造熔爐，並於日本福島增設玻纖布生產工
廠。雖 Nittobo 於 2028 年度產能將為 2025 年度之 3 倍，惟觀察產品需求已
非當前擴產速度得以消化。Nittobo 於 11/05/2026 財報說明會上表示，將投
資 200 億日元，擴充日本福島縣與台灣營運據點之生產設備，增產 T-glass，
目標 2029 年度產能擴增至 2025 年度之 3.6 倍。 
產品報價方面，富喬於 06/26/2026 調漲 E 布報價 30%。大陸 CCL 龍頭廠建
滔積層板 04/28/2026 發布漲價通知，宣布即日起 FR-4 銅箔基板、半固化片
（ PP）將再調漲 10%。 05/27/2026 再調漲板料報價 10%、 PP 調漲 20%。
07/06/2026 分別再次調漲 FR-4、CEM-1/22F 及 PP 等產品報價約 15%、10%、
15%。08/28/2026 分別調漲 FR-4、7628(含 )以上厚布、7628 以下薄布約 10%、
10%、 20%。為因應市場強勁需求，富喬、建滔積層板 08/2026 後仍持續調
漲產品報價。 
3Q26 毛利率再創高，QoQ+8.27%至 31.60%：  
台玻 3Q26 受惠於 Low Dk2 、 Low CTE 等高毛利產品出貨比重拉升，帶動
3Q26 營業毛利 QoQ+58.80%、 YoY+257.07%，且毛利率較 2Q26 提升 8.27%
至 31.60%，亦優於原預期之 26.23%。因此 3Q26 稅後純益 27.05 億元、稅
後 EPS 0.93 元，均優於原預期之 20.47 億元、0.70 元。1~3Q26 台玻累計營
收達 364.41 億元，YoY+18.69%，毛利率 25.38%，稅後純益 45.66 億元，
YoY 由虧轉盈，稅後 EPS 1.57 元。 
10/2026 營收 MoM+12.04%、 YoY+56.49%：  
台玻 10/2026 營收 55.66 億元，MoM+12.04%、 YoY+56.49%，優於預期，與
原預期值 52.21 億元，差異約+6.2%。主因玻纖布事業營收持續攀升，整體
出貨動能較 09/2026 成長，部分抵銷中國區域平板玻璃尚未損平影響。以研
究部預期當前玻纖布營收比重約 40~46%，高階玻纖布占其中 40~42%推估，
10/2026 高階玻纖布營收貢獻約 MoM+13~16%。  
不只有 Low Dk/Low CTE， Q 布也為台玻戰略儲備： 
2027~2028 年伴隨 AI 伺服器升級及機櫃出貨增加下，CCL 板材有望大量導
入 M9 等高階材料，並搭配 Q 布或 Low Dk2 等混合配方，且台玻亦具 Q 布
接單出貨能力。 
日前台玻參展 TPCA Show，展出 Q 布玻纖紗，顯示其 Q 布技術儲備，大秀
高階肌肉。惟觀察 AI 伺服器規格調整，預期 2027 年產品需求仍以 Low Dk
系列玻纖布為主，大量搭載 Q 布尚早。不同材料規格之玻纖布，其製程方
式雖雷同，然配方均有差異，窯爐使用亦不同，故產能簽訂後，供應鏈替換
廠商可能性不高。預期台玻將視下游廠商產品需求，決議 Q 布生產時程。

### Page 4

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

4 群益投顧 
預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 比重仍高於 Low CTE：  
台玻 E-glass 轉至中國產銷，當前高階玻纖布以 Low Dk2/Low CTE 為主要
營收來源。Low CTE 已於 2025 年底開始小量出貨，目前產品出貨量已較
2026 年初顯著增加，預期營收佔比將於 2027 年持續提升，並於 2H27~2028
年新產能開出後，追趕 Low Dk2。目前下游終端需求仍以 Low Dk 產品為主
力，預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 營收比重仍將高於 Low Dk1 及 Low CTE。  
積極擴建 Low Dk/Low CTE 產能，期待擴產效益顯現，預估 2027 年稅後
EPS 4.84 元： 
產能布局方面，05/11/2026 經董事會宣布，為因應市場對於高階玻纖布需求
上揚，新增資本支出 22.42 億元，用以擴建公司桃園廠及鹿港廠窯爐及冷修
案，預計主要用於增加 Low Dk2、 T-glass 產能、提高市佔率，最快 2Q27 投
產。06/12/2026 股東會董事長指出，2026 年預計再增加 20 億元資本支出，
用於擴充 Low Dk(低介電損耗)、Low CTE(低熱膨脹係數)之 T 布產能，並表
示產品供不應求將延續至 2027 年底。目前高階玻纖布擴產計畫共分三期，
第一期已於 2026 年投入並量產，第二期預計 2026 年完成，新增之 20 億元
為第三期投資計畫，預計 2027 年完工。11/02/2026 董事會通過桃園廠、鹿
港廠窯爐增建及子公司台嘉蚌埠玻璃纖維有限公司冷修擴充案，預估投入
金額約新台幣 16.3 億元及人民幣 11.5 億元。 
產品報價方面，台玻始終秉持於維繫良好客戶關係，並以客戶需求為營運宗
旨。雖 2025~2026 年以來 E-glass 報價倍數增長，惟基於多年來與客戶合作
建立之信任及當前產品利潤貢獻尚屬可控範圍，故而預期將不急於大幅反
映產品報價，但亦不排除部分調整產品價格。 
預期 2027 年 Low Dk2/Low CTE玻纖布出貨量仍將較 2026 年提升，Low Dk1
營收貢獻亦將延續動能。台玻 Low CTE 產品已取得國內載板大廠認證，訂
單能見度已達 2027 年，且 2028 年訂單洽談順暢。若供應鏈產品需求無較大
質變，2028 年玻纖布事業群仍將維持成長格局。預估台玻 4Q26 底 ~2027 年
玻纖布營收比重可望超越平板玻璃及玻璃器皿。研究部預估台玻 2026 年營
收 534.01 億元，YoY+28.70%，毛利率 28.28%，稅後純益 83.19 億元，YoY
虧轉盈，稅後 EPS 2.86 元；2027 年營收 711.51 億元，YoY+33.24%，毛利
率 34.43%，稅後純益 140.68 億元，YoY+69.11%，稅後 EPS 4.84 元。 
圖二、台玻季營收變化預估

資料來源：台玻，群益投顧預估彙整

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

全球经济温和复苏，价格带动进口增速反超出口
——2026 年 10 月进出口数据及全球经济点评
事件：2026年10月，我国进出口总额为7193.8亿美元，同比
增长 37.9%。其中，出口金额为 4123.3 亿美元，同比增长
35.2%；进口金额为 3070.5 亿美元，同比增长 41.6%；贸易顺
差为 1052.8 亿美元，同比增长 19.6%。
一、10月进出口数据解析
 10 月出口延续高位增长，海外制造业扩张和科技产业链需求

构成主要支撑。10 月出口同比增长 35.2%（以美元计价，下
同），较 9 月小幅放缓 1.1 个百分点，但仍明显高于 2021—
2025年10月约5.9%的平均增速。从外部环境看，10月全球制
造业PMI为52.6%，连续15个月处于扩张区间，全球制造业仍
处上行周期为短期商品贸易提供支撑。值得注意的是，本轮制
造业扩张更多由AI、国防等领域资本开支以及投资品和中间品
需求驱动，相关需求对价格和融资成本的敏感度相对较低，因
而在高利率和通胀压力下仍保持较强韧性。与此同时，能源及
物流扰动导致全球供应商交付周期延长、积压订单增加，部分
经济体面临产能和原材料约束，我国产业链完整、生产响应较
快和交付能力稳定的优势进一步显现，有利于承接全球订单。
此外，去年同期出口基数偏低，也放大了本月同比增幅。
 亚洲区域贸易保持活力，韩国和拉美市场成为本月较为突出
的增量来源。10 月我国对东盟出口同比增长 46.3%，占出口
总额的比重升至 18.9%，对整体出口增速拉动约 8.1 个百分
点，仍是出口增长的最大拉动项。 亚洲产业链内部出现一

定分化，其中对韩国出口增速由 9 月的 68.2%进一步升至
92.4%，拉动出口增长约 3.3 个百分点，是本月主要市场中
最突出的加速项；对中国香港和印度出口分别增长 48.6%和
43.7%，继续保持较快增长。对美欧出口仍具韧性，对美国www.ccxi.com.cn
进出口数据点评2026 年 11 月 10 日 2026 年第 62 期

海外经济分化加深下外贸韧性能维持多久？
——2026 年 8 月进出口数据点评
和欧盟出口分别增长32.9%和24.3%，合计拉动整体出口增长约7.3个百分点，但增
速较 9 月均有所放缓。与此同时，新兴市场的支撑作用进一步增强，对拉丁美洲出
口增长 32.1%，较 9 月加快 7.4 个百分点，其中对巴西出口增速由 22.3%升至 42.0%；
对非洲出口增长 19.5%，也较上月有所加快。总体看，10 月出口增长仍具有较强的
市场广度，东盟继续发挥“压舱石”作用，韩国和拉美市场则接续提供新增动力，
市场多元化在对冲部分传统市场增速放缓方面发挥了积极作用。
 科技产品的主导作用进一步增强，算力设备拉动上升，汽车和传统商品贡献则有所
减弱。10 月机电产品出口同比增长 46.1%，占出口总额的 67.4%，对整体出口增速
拉动 28.7 个百分点，较9 月提高1.1 个百分点；高新技术产品出口增长 66.6%，拉
动出口增长 18.1 个百分点，较 9 月提高 0.7 个百分点。其中，集成电路出口增长

137.1%，拉动出口增长约 7.5 个百分点，仍是最主要的细分支撑；自动数据处理设
备及其零部件出口增长 114.8%，拉动作用由 9 月的 5.1 个百分点升至 6.1 个百分点，
成为本月科技产品中较为突出的增量来源。相比之下，汽车及底盘出口增速由
62.0%回落至 37.6%，拉动作用由 2.4 个百分点降至 1.8 个百分点；服装、纺织品、
玩具、家用电器、塑料制品和家具出口虽然均保持两位数增长，但合计拉动由 9 月
的约 3.8 个百分点降至2.8 个百分点。总体看，10 月出口高增更多依靠科技产业链
支撑，算力设备的拉动作用进一步增强，而汽车和传统商品贡献边际减弱，出口增
长动力较 9 月有所集中。

海外经济分化加深下外贸韧性能维持多久？
——2026 年 8 月进出口数据点评
 价格因素推动进口增速大幅上升，科技产业链与能源商品共同推动进口增速超过出口。
10月进口同比增长41.6%，较9月加快10.6个百分点，创五年来新高。在出口仍保持
35%以上增长的情况下，进口进一步提速，推动当月贸易顺差由 9 月的 1335.3 亿美元
收窄至1052.8亿美元，减少282.5亿美元。值得注意的是，尽管今年以来进口增速持
续走高，但商品结构集中度也同步上升，且价格对进口总额的带动明显强于规模，能
源品与中间品进口的增多并不代表内需的改善。10 月进口增长动力明显向能源商品扩
展，呈现“科技产业链保持强劲、能源商品拉动跃升”的特征。不过，10 月国内制造
业 PMI 重新回落至收缩区间，铁矿石、农产品等与传统生产和消费需求关联较强的商
品进口仍较弱，内需尚未全面回暖。
 高技术产品仍是进口增长的基础性支撑，能源商品则贡献了本月进口加速的主要
边际增量。10 月高新技术产品进口同比增长 72.1%，对整体进口增速拉动 23.3 个

百分点，较9月提高约2.0个百分点。其中，集成电路进口增长78.3%，拉动进口
增长 13.7 个百分点，较9 月提高约0.8 个百分点；自动数据处理设备及其零部件
进口增长 261.0%，拉动约 8.0 个百分点，虽较 9 月略有减弱，但仍处于较高水平。
更值得关注的是，能源商品对进口增长的合计拉动由 9 月的约 1.3 个百分点大幅
升至 6.8 个百分点，是进口增速明显加快的主要原因。其中，原油进口金额由 9
月增长 3.3%升至增长 41.0%，拉动作用由 0.3 个百分点升至 4.6 个百分点；天然
气、煤炭和成品油分别拉动进口增长约 1.0、0.8 和 0.4 个百分点。与此同时，铁

海外经济分化加深下外贸韧性能维持多久？
——2026 年 8 月进出口数据点评
议升温，我国科技产品出口对全球科技投资周期的依赖进一步加深，而汽车及传统商品
增长动力有所减弱，出口增长的持续性面临一定考验。贸易政策方面，美国中期选举后
国内政策博弈可能加剧，现有关税安排仍具有阶段性，主要经济体针对重点产业的贸易
限制和供应链本地化趋势亦难以逆转。此外，中东局势反复及能源运输风险仍可能推高
生产和物流成本，进一步压缩企业利润空间。总体看，短期出口仍具较强韧性，但后续
能否保持较快增长，将更多取决于科技投资景气的持续性、传统商品需求恢复及外部贸
易环境的变化。
二、全球经济跟踪：全球经济保持温和增长，通胀黏性与政策不确定性加剧市场调整
近一个月，从宏观经济表现来看，美国经济增长加快，欧洲温和复苏，日本内需仍然偏
弱。通胀黏性与科技投资不确定性成为市场关注重点，一方面，继 9 月美联储加息后，11月
英国央行进一步加息控制通胀水平。另一方面，AI 投资回报担忧引发科技股明显调整，新兴

市场资本外流扩大。全球贸易受AI 产业链需求支撑保持韧性，但能源运输风险和地区分化仍
然突出。此外，11 月 3 日，美国中期选举民主党夺取国会两院控制权，特朗普政府施政面临
更多制约，也给外部环境带来新变局。总体看，全球经济尚具韧性，但增长动力集中、通胀
反弹及政策约束加大，经济金融运行的不确定性进一步上升。
 主要经济体增长分化，能源冲击推动生产端通胀加快上行，货币政策面临更大约
束。美国经济保持较强韧性，三季度 GDP 环比折年实际增长 3.4%，较二季度加快
1.2 个百分点，消费和投资共同支撑增长，其中 AI 相关资本开支持续扩张，但对
就业及居民收入的带动相对有限。9 月 CPI 同比上涨 3.7%，能源价格上涨加大通
胀压力。继 9 月加息 25 个基点后，美联储 10 月维持政策利率在 3.75%—4.00%，
两名委员主张进一步加息，反映通胀风险仍受到高度关注。11 月 3 日中期选举中，
民主党赢得参众两院多数席位，选民对高生活成本和中东战争的不满集中显现。
共和党失去国会控制权后，特朗普政府推动新增财政刺激、税收和其他立法议程

的难度上升，但关税及对外政策仍具有较强行政裁量空间，后续政策博弈可能加
剧经济不确定性。欧洲经济温和修复，日本内需仍显疲弱，通胀压力使得欧、日
央行货币政策维持偏紧。三季度欧元区 GDP 环比增长 0.4%，同比增长1.3%，德国
小幅回升，法国表现偏弱。10 月欧元区通胀升至 4.0%，其中能源价格同比上涨
20.8%，加剧居民购买力和企业成本压力。日本9月核心CPI同比上涨2.1%，居民

海外经济分化加深下外贸韧性能维持多久？
——2026 年 8 月进出口数据点评
消费恢复缓慢，日本央行 10 月维持政策利率 1.25%不变。总体看，中东能源冲击
使主要央行面临更突出的增长与通胀权衡，货币政策由此前的宽松预期转向维持
限制性立场甚至进一步紧缩，尤其加大了能源进口依赖型经济体的滞胀压力。
 全球金融市场波动加剧，AI 投资回报担忧引发科技股调整，能源价格与利率预期
变化推动资产重新定价。近一个月，金融市场呈现明显的结构性分化。10 月中旬
至今，标普 500 指数下跌约 3.3%，纳斯达克指数下跌约 7.7%，道琼斯指数则上涨
约 1.4%，反映资金由高估值科技股向价值和防御性板块轮动。尽管美国企业盈利
总体保持韧性，但市场对 AI 资本开支持续扩张、债务融资增加及投资回报兑现的
担忧升温，美国中期选举后数据中心建设政策的不确定性进一步加剧市场调整。
债券市场方面，避险需求上升推动美国 10 年期国债收益率由 5.2%左右降至当前
的 4.86%。大宗商品方面，中东局势反复推动油价先涨后跌，布伦特原油 10 月上

涨约13%，11月初随着地缘冲突缓和预期升温明显回落，当前降至约92美元/桶；
黄金亦呈现较大波动。总体看，近期全球资产价格调整是高利率环境下 AI 投资回
报、能源供应风险及政策不确定性共同推动的结构性重新定价。
 全球金融条件偏紧与科技股调整加剧新兴市场资本外流，但我国跨境资金仍保持
净流入。IIF 数据显示，10 月新兴市场证券投资净流出 338 亿美元，较 9 月修订
后的 193 亿美元进一步扩大，为 3 月以来最大单月流出规模，与上年同期净流入
274 亿美元形成鲜明对比。亚洲科技股调整、美元相对强势及主要经济体利率维
持高位，共同加大新兴市场资本流出压力。相比之下，我国跨境资金流动总体稳
健。国家外汇管理局数据显示，9月银行代客涉外收付款顺差407亿美元，连续六
个月保持净流入，但较8月的624亿美元下降约35%；同期银行结售汇顺差562亿
美元，反映境内外汇供求总体平衡、资金流入仍具支撑。结构上，货物贸易项下
资金保持较大规模净流入，但企业对外直接投资及境外关联企业融资有所增加。

总体看，外部金融环境收紧对我国跨境资金流动形成一定扰动，但贸易顺差及相
对稳定的市场预期仍提供支撑，需关注后续资本流入放缓及汇率波动风险。
 中东冲突仍在持续博弈拉锯中，能源运输和全球供应链仍面临较大不确定性。10
月以来，美伊冲突持续围绕霍尔木兹海峡及周边能源设施展开，11 月初多艘商船
遇袭，但卡塔尔等国也重启斡旋，局势出现一定缓和迹象。与此同时，也门政府

Document 104: 2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 2 日

1/3 
2027 年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート 
～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 経団連による、2027 年春闘に向けての経労委報告原案が判明した。高い賃上げの「社会全体
への浸透」を掲げており、大企業を中心に高い賃上げが定着しつつあるなか、その動きを中
小企業などにも広げることに重点を置く姿勢がうかがえる。また、筒井経団連会長の発言か
らは、ベア実施の検討をスタンダードとする従来の方針が維持されていることも確認できる。 
○ 流通やサービス、繊維などの労働組合で構成されるＵＡゼンセンも定昇込み６％、ベア４％
など、前年並みの高い賃上げ要求を掲げた。労使とも、賃上げに向けての姿勢にトーンダウ
ンはみられない。 
○ 一方、賃上げ率のさらなる加速を示唆する材料は現時点で多くない。連合の要求目安は前年
より強まったものの、経団連は賃上げ率の加速までは示していないとみられ、ＵＡゼンセン
の要求率は前年と同水準である。2027 年春闘では、５％近傍という高い賃上げ率を維持でき
るかが焦点となる。 
○ 日銀の植田総裁は、2027 年春闘で２％のインフレ率を前提とした賃金設定が行われることの
重要性を指摘した。2026 年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍化するなかでも高い賃上げが続くかど
うかは、２％の物価安定目標の定着や今後の金融政策を判断するうえで重要となる。

経労委報告原案は、高い賃上げの「社会全体への浸透」を掲げる 
10 月22 日のレポート（2027 年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも
昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所）では、連
合の基本構想や経団連の経労委報告骨子案などをもとに、2027 年春闘に向けた賃上げ姿勢は労使
双方とも前年からのトーンダウンはみられないと評価したが、その後、経団連の経労委報告原案
やＵＡゼンセンの要求方針素案が明らかになった。本稿では、これらの新たな材料を踏まえ、春
闘の初動のモメンタムをアップデートする。 
10 月30 日、経団連が 2027 年春闘に向けて取りまとめる「経営労働政策特別委員会報告（経労
委報告）」の原案内容が報道された。報道によれば、原案では賃上げの力強いモメンタムを継続

### Page 2

2/3 
し、その動きを社会全体に浸透させるために取り組みを強化する方針が示された。特に、中小企
業が継続的・計画的な賃上げを実施できるかが重要とされ、適正な価格転嫁に加え、大企業が持
つ人材や技術を活用して中小企業の収益力向上を支援する必要性が指摘された。また、ＡＩの普
及を踏まえ、働き手が生み出した成果をどのように評価し、賃金などの処遇へ反映するかについ
て、見直しを求めている。生産性向上によって生み出された付加価値を賃金にどう配分するか
は、今後の労使交渉においても重要な課題となるだろう。 
経団連はこれまで、2023 年を賃上げの「起点」、2024 年を「加速」、2025 年を「定着」、2026 年
を「さらなる定着」の年と位置付けてきた。今回の原案では「社会全体への浸透」が掲げられて
おり、大企業を中心に高い賃上げが定着しつつあるなか、その動きを中小企業などにも広げるこ
とに重点を置く姿勢がうかがえる。2026 年春闘向けの経労委報告でも、中小企業における構造的
な賃上げや適正な価格転嫁の重要性が強調されていたが、今回の原案では、大企業による人材・
技術面での支援にも言及するなど、賃上げの裾野を広げるための取り組みをより具体的に示して
いる。 
賃上げに対する経営側の基本姿勢も維持されている。2026 年春闘向けの経労委報告では、ベア
実施の検討を賃金交渉のスタンダードと位置付けていたが、筒井経団連会長は今年９月７日の記
者会見でも、「ベースアップ実施の検討を引き続きスタンダードと位置付けていきたい」と述べて
いる。ベアに対する従来の方針が維持されていることは重要だ。今春以降のイラン情勢悪化を受
け、原材料費や物流費の上昇によって企業の賃上げ余力が圧迫されることが懸念されていたが、
少なくとも現時点で経団連が賃上げに慎重な姿勢へ転じた様子はうかがえない。

UAゼンセンも前年並みの高い要求を維持 
労働側でも、主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針が明らかになり始めた。10 月 27 日、流通やサ
ービス、繊維などの労働組合で構成されるＵＡゼンセンは、2027 年春闘の要求方針素案を公表し
た。正社員組合員については、定昇込みで６％、ベア等の賃上げ分で４％を基本とし、賃金水準
が最低基準に満たない組合などでは格差是正分を加えて７％の賃上げを要求する。また、短時間
組合員については、制度昇給分に加えて５％、制度昇給分が明確でない場合には７％を目安とす
るとのことである。 
これらの要求水準は、いずれも 2026 年春闘から変わっていない。６～７％という要求水準は依
然として高いが、要求率を前年からさらに引き上げたわけではないことには注意が必要だ。連合
は 2027 年春闘に向けた基本構想で「６％中心、５％以上」と前年より強い要求目安を示したが、
実際には前年の段階で、すでに６％程度の賃上げを要求していた産別組織もある。今回の連合の
方針には、こうした既存の要求水準を追認する側面もあり、ＵＡゼンセンの要求方針はその一例
といえる。

前年からのトーンダウンはみられていないが、加速しているわけでもない  
以上を踏まえると、2027 年春闘の初動のモメンタムは引き続き良好と評価できる。経団連はベ

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ア実施の検討をスタンダードとする従来の方針を維持し、高い賃上げを中小企業などにも広げる
ことで「社会全体への浸透」を目指す姿勢を示している。労働側でも、連合が前年より強い要求
目安を掲げており、ＵＡゼンセンも前年並みの高い要求水準を維持した。イラン情勢悪化に伴う
コスト増加が懸念されるなかでも、労使双方の賃上げ姿勢に前年からのトーンダウンはみられな
い。 
もっとも、賃上げ姿勢が慎重化していないことと、賃上げ率がさらに高まることは別問題であ
る。経団連が重視しているのは高い賃上げの継続とその裾野の拡大であり、賃上げ率の加速を示
唆しているわけではないだろう。また、ＵＡゼンセンの賃上げ要求は高いが、要求率自体は前年
から据え置かれている。連合の要求目安は強まったものの、現時点で賃上げ率の加速を見込むだ
けの材料は揃っていない。エコノミストの予想を集計するＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査（10 月調
査）でも、2027 年春闘の賃上げ率予想は4.96％とされており、前年実績の 5.18％から小幅に鈍化
するとの見方となっている（数値は厚生労働省ベース）。 
当面の焦点は、５％近傍という高い賃上げ率を維持できるかどうかにある。もっとも、今後の
経済・物価情勢によっては企業の賃上げ余力や労働側の要求姿勢も変化し得るため、先行きを慎
重に見極める必要がある。当面、自動車総連や電機連合など主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針
が前年からどの程度変化するかが注目される。 
金融政策の観点からも2027 年春闘の動向は重要である。日銀の植田総裁は 10 月 30 日の記者会
見で、基調的な物価上昇率が２％の物価安定の目標と概ね整合的な水準に達したとの認識を示し
た上で、2027 年春闘について、労使交渉でも２％のインフレ率が前提として織り込まれることが
必要との考えを示した。一方、2026 年の物価上昇率が過去２～３年より低くなる可能性を指摘
し、それが春闘の結果にどう影響するかを注視する姿勢も示している。 
ここ数年の春闘では、物価上昇に負けない賃上げを実現することが、高い賃上げ要求を支える
重要な要因となってきた。2026 年の物価上昇率は 2025 年と比べれば鈍化する見込みであり、こう
した観点からの賃上げ圧力はやや弱まる可能性がある。 
もっとも、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを前提として賃金を設定するよう
になれば、足元の物価上昇率が低下しても安定的な賃上げは持続しやすくなる。日銀は基調的な
物価上昇率が２％に「到達」したと判断したが、２％の物価安定目標が「定着」したとまでは判
断していない。その意味でも、2027 年春闘は、２％の物価上昇を前提とした賃金設定がどの程度
定着しているかを確認する機会となる。春闘に向けた労使双方の姿勢や実際の賃上げ動向は、今
後の日銀の金融政策判断においても重要な材料となるだろう。

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
ASMedia Technology Inc
Inventory adjustment weighs on N-T outlook
Q326 earnings broadly in line; PC demand remains weak
ASMedia reported Q326 EPS of NT$26.39, broadly in line with UBSe but above Street,
helped by ~NT$1.5bn of non-op income mainly from WT Micro. Q326 sales of NT
$2.66bn declined 2% QoQ and 33% YoY, reflecting continued weakness in PC demand
amid elevated memory prices and the shift at its major customer from AM5 back to the

lower-cost AM4 platform. Q326 GM came in at 51.6% (+1.3ppt QoQ), helped by a
more favorable product mix and pricing adjustments.  Opex increased to NT$795mn on
higher R&D spending including 12nm chip tape-out and a 10-15% increase in
headcount expansion, resulting in  OpM declining 2.1ppt QoQ to 21.7%.  Q326 sales mix
was 74% for host controllers, ~21% automotive and ~5%  device.
Sell-through improving, but  inventory adjustment to weigh through Q127F

ASMedia is seeing improving PC motherboard demand, with Q326 motherboard
production up >20% QoQ and management viewing H126 as the near-term trough.
However, elevated memory prices have driven AMD's product mix back toward the
lower-ASP AM4 platform (AM4  >70% of Q326 chipset shipments up from >50% in
Q226), while AMD continues to digest inventory. It noted AMD's sales contribution has
already declined from >50% of sales in H126 to <50% in Q326, and management

expects the inventory correction to continue through Q127, delaying the benefit from
improving motherboard sell-through. We expect October to mark the trough (monthly
sales down 43% MoM) and will remain muted on AMD's inventory control, resulting  in
Q426 sales  declining~33% QoQ  while GM should decline by 1.6ppt QoQ to 50.0% as
slower pull in of AM5 could lead to inventory provision, despite a reversal in H127E.
2027E recovery more back-end-loaded

ASMedia remains confident in retaining its sole-supplier position for AMD’s next-
generation Olympic (AM5.5) chipset platform, with contribution expected from H227
and ASP ~50-60% higher than AM4. We expect USB4 demand to recover alongside the
Olympic ramp, with additional upside from a new US robotics customer from H227.
Separately, we expect AM5 shipments to recover  and potential  inventory writebacks to
provide upside to GM. We believe PCIe packet-switch contribution could be more

gradual given slower design-in progress. Overall, we estimate ASMedia’s 2027E sales to
grow ~5% YoY (vs. previous 17% YoY expectations), with growth skewed to H227.
Valuation: Retain Buy with PT of NT$1,500
We lower our 2026/27/28E EPS by 2%/1%/2% primarily on softer PC demand and
inventory adjustment, offset by higher better earnings contribution from WT Micro. We
cut our PT to NT$1,500 (from NT$1,700), based on our SOTP valuation  (18x for core

earnings and 11.5x for non-core), implying 13x 2027E PE.  Maintain Buy rating as stock is
trading near trough 14x NTM PE  vs an 11-48x historical range, while its diversification
into non-PC   should support its topline/OP to recover from 2027E.

Company Description
ASMedia is a Taiwan-based fabless IC design house founded by parent company Asustek
Computer Inc. in 2004. ASMedia is dedicated to high-speed input/output (I/O) controllers,
such as USB, PCIe and SATA, and its products are mainly used in desktop PCs, motherboards

and add-on cards. In 2024, ASMedia generated 84% of its revenue from host controllers and
16% from device controllers. The company collaborates with Advanced Micro Devices (AMD)
to design southbridge chipsets for Zen-based desktop central processing units (CPUs). AMD is
the company's largest client account, contributing 58% of revenue in 2024.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We base our price target on sum-of-the-parts methodology. We value ASMedia's core and

non-core businesses based on a target PE multiple.
Fabless integrated circuit (IC) design houses face a variety of uncertainty, including rapidly
changing technology, end-market demand, competition, employee turnover and foundry
wafer costs. They are also vulnerable to legal disputes over intellectual property rights.
We believe risks for ASMedia include: 1) AMD's chipset insourcing or other design houses
entering AMD's chipset business; 2) faster penetration of USB4 native support in chipsets; 3)

more entrants in the USB device controller market; 4) continued suspension of China tender
projects; and 5) increased US export controls impacting China's computing capability.

market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR) Stocks may be flagged
by the analyst to indicate that the stock's price target and/or rating may change in the near term, usually in response to an event that

may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have a 12-month investment horizon.

such as structure, management, performance record and discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management,
performance record and discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record and discount. Core

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

We hosted NYPCB’s 3Q26 post result on November 9th. Key 
takeaways include: 
(1) NYPCB mentioned its 4Q26 GM on ABF substrate already 
exceeds 40% (vs. ~15% in 2025 and 25% in 1H26) driven mainly by 
the solid high-end product demand (which contributed 50%+ of the 
ABF revenue in 2026 vs. <20% in 2025), while the company 
mentioned it will gain more high-end market share in 2027 which 
should further drive company’s overall GM level.  
(2) For the new high-end projects in 2027, NYPCB mentioned it will 
gain significant share in the key US CSP next-gen AI project, and 
high-end server CPU project. Also, the company mentioned it 
expects a proactive adoption on 1.6T Ethernet switch demand 
driven by CSPs investment in AI infrastructure, driving its high-end 
switch substrate ASP (the upgrade on substrate, from 800G to 1.6T, 
suggesting a 50%+ higher substrate unit price, per GSe).  
(3) In terms of the ABF substrate pricing, NYPCB believes its ABF 
substrate ASP will further go up by 10-15% QoQ in 4Q26, while it 
also expects the pricing hike ratio to be at least maintained at this 
level from 1Q27-4Q27, without seeing much downside risk. 
Moreover, the company believes the ASP growth could be even 
faster than its expectation if the high-end orders accelerate in 
coming quarters or if they see an even tigher S/D outlook for the 
overall ABF substrate industry.  
(4) In terms of the BT substrate pricing outlook, the company 
mentioned the pricing hike ratio will be low single digits % QoQ in 
4Q26, which will further trend up in 1Q27-4Q27, with a high 
confidence level on the shortage of BT substrate in coming quarters.  
(4) In terms of 4Q26 revenue guidance, the company is expecting 
~20% QoQ revenue growth, driven mainly by the high-end ABF 
projects ramp up + new AI project ramps + BT substrate pricing hike

(low single digits % QoQ); moreover, the company expects its 1Q27 revenue to be flat 
QoQ, despite the ~20% less working days. For GM, the company expects 4Q26 GM to be 
low to mid 30% which should further improve in 1Q27.  
(5) The company mentioned it is working on a new capacity investment plan for 2028, 
but has not yet finalized the plan. In terms of the funding method, NYPCB mentioned it 
will not do any equity based fundraising, but will rely on internal cash or bank loan.  
(6) NYPCB also mentioned it will focus only on CoPoS technology, whose substrate 
pricing is also US$1000+ (vs. US$100-200 for the most high end ABF substrate now), 
while the product demand could start ramping up from 2028 and start contributing 
significant revenue from 2029. For EMIB-T, NYPCB believes the penetration rate could 
only be 10% in the foreseeable future, and it has no plan to invest in this small market.  
We maintain our positive view on the overall ABF substrate industry, with 34%/51% 
shortage ratio in 2027/28 (see here), and believe such high shortage ratio will protect 
companies’ GM/OPM even if we start to see some slowdown on AI CAPEX in coming 
years. We started to see all ABF substrate players’ GM surpass 40% in 4Q26, which is 
much faster than our original expectation (Taiwan ABF substrate suppliers reach 40%+ 
GM at the 6-7th quarters of the shortage in last upcycle, but it only takes 3-4 quarters 
for Taiwan players to hit the 40%+ GM, driven mainly by the solid AI upgrading demand 
this time). We expect NYPCB’s ABF substrate GM could reach historical peak level 
(55-60%) in 2H27, suggesting a fruitful upside for the EPS growth in coming years (we 
expect NYPCB’s EPS to grow by 150%+ 2025-29E CAGR), and believe the ABF substrate 
will continue to be a key bottleneck of the overall semi/tech supply chain in the next 3-5 
years.  
Maintain Buy on NYPCB, as the key spot price ABF substrate supplier in the market 
which should benefit strongly from the S/D shortage and pricing hike in the 
foreseeable future, with an unchanged TP of NT$2,700 (based on 21x 2028E EPS).  
Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - NYPCB 
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit 
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB 
considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate 
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the 
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the 
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end 
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding 
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s 
high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook 
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger 
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the 
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long 
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s 
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148% 
2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s

Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, and (3)
Slower-than-expected NYPCB new high-end capacity qualification progress.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

⾼盛认为，美元短期内仍有上⾏空间，但 2027 年将迎来实质性逆转。⽀撑这轮涨势的核⼼
驱动⼒正是 AI 相关投资预期的持续升温，⽽⼀旦这⼀预期⻅顶，美元也将⾯临同样的拐
点。
摘要：
美元与 AI 资本开⽀预期深度绑定，⾼盛警告：当 AI 叙事退潮，美元将随之承压。
⾼盛外汇策略团队在《 2027 全球外汇展望》报告中指出，美元短期内仍有上⾏空间，但 2027 年
将迎来实质性逆转。在历经 2025 年的⼤规模抛售与多元化配置后，美元于 2026 年悄然收复失地
—— 欧元兑美元回落⾄ " 解放⽇ " 以来低位，美元指数（ DXY ）触及 2025 年 4 ⽉以来最⾼点，美元
兑⽇元逼近 160 关⼝。⾼盛认为，⽀撑这轮涨势的核⼼驱动⼒正是 AI 相关投资预期的持续升温，
⽽⼀旦这⼀预期⻅顶，美元也将⾯临同样的拐点。
数据显⽰，外国投资者在截⾄今年 8 ⽉的⼀年间净购⼊创纪录的 1.47 万亿美元美国股票及企业债
券，⼏乎是全球⾦融危机前峰值的两倍。外资持有的美国股票中，约 90% 为账⾯浮盈⽽⾮实际

增量买⼊，⽽对冲⽐率处于历史低位 —— 换⾔之，仅对冲⽐率的结构性上移，就⾜以在⽆需⼤
规模资本外流的情况下对美元形成实质性压制。⾼盛警告， AI 资本开⽀预期⼀旦显现⻅顶迹
象，投资者可能要求更⼤的价格折让 —— 包括汇率层⾯的让步。
美元重回⾼估区间， AI 成为核⼼⽀撑变量
美元估值已重回历史偏⾼⽔平。⾼盛汇率错配模型显⽰，贸易加权美元较合理价值⾼估约
13% ，较 9 ⽉时的 7% ⼤幅上升；对亚洲货币的⾼估幅度则始终维持在 25% 左右，从未真正收
窄。
⾼盛策略师 Kamakshya Trivedi 与 Michael Cahill 在报告中明确将美元定性为 "AI 交易 " ，" 美元表
现与 AI 相关股票表现⾼度挂钩 —— ⽆论⾏情好坏皆然。 " 这⼀判断背后有数据⽀撑：在外国资本
流⼊美国的结构中，私⼈购买美国企业股票的⽐例已飙升⾄ 40 年历史区间的顶端。
值得注意的是， 2026 年外汇市场呈现出⼀个罕⻅悖论：全年外汇持仓变动幅度创历史之最，但
实际汇率波动区间却极为狭窄。⾼盛将这⼀现象归结为低衰退⻛险与有限政策分化并存的市场

环境，使得套息交易主导全年，⽅向性押注则普遍受挫。

### Page 2

美联储鹰派⽴场为美元提供短期⽀撑
⾼盛并不建议当下即做空美元，原因在于美联储持续偏鹰的政策信号。在最新通胀数据显著超
预期 ——70 位预测⼈⼠中仅⼀位预测值更⾼ —— 后，⾼盛经济学家将今年加息次数上调⾄三
次。
10 年期美债收益率已重返 5% 上⽅，最新 PPI 数据创四年新⾼，均构成对美元的阶段性⽀撑。⾼
盛基准预测认为，通胀最终将趋于温和，美联储实际收紧幅度将远⼩于市场当前定价，届时美
元将承压。但该⾏坦承，近期⻛险仍 " 偏向美元⾛强 " ，尤其若美国⾦融条件收紧的外溢效应进⼀
步暴露欧洲脆弱性。
就欧元⽽⾔，⾼盛预测欧元兑美元将在三个⽉内跌⾄ 1.08 ，主因能源冲击带来的贸易条件恶化效
应滞后传导，以及法国上半年经济接近停滞（该预测低于市场普遍预期）；随后于 2027 年底反弹
⾄ 1.15 。⾼盛同时指出，法国的可信财政整固措施可能要等到 6 ⽉议会选举之后才能落地，⽽此
类措施往往需要市场压⼒作为政治推⼿。
⽇元：低估 30% ，政策转向提供结构性⽀撑
如果说美元是 AI 交易的多头，⽇元则是⾼盛设定的 AI 对冲⼯具。按照该⾏⾸选的公允价值模型，

⽇元对美元低估幅度约达 30% ，是 G10 货币中估值偏差最为显著的货币之⼀。
政策层⾯，⽇本央⾏的⽴场正在切实转变。⾼盛经济学家预计，⽇本央⾏将于 2027 年 6 ⽉前将政
策利率升⾄ 2% ，意味着还有三次 25 个基点的加息，⾼于市场⽬前约 1.75% 的隐含定价。该⾏还
注意到，⾃ 10 ⽉初以来，在纳斯达克跑输标普 500 指数逾 1% 的交易⽇内，⽇元平均涨幅约为
0.3% ，是 G10 表现最佳的货币。
⾼盛对美元兑⽇元的路径预测为：三个⽉ 158 、六个⽉ 155 、⼗⼆个⽉ 150 ，较当前约 160 的⽔平
下跌约 6% 。该⾏同时警⽰， " 若市场对 AI 预期的质疑更为显著，调整幅度可能⼤幅超出上述预
测 " 。
AI 债务泡沫与对冲⽐率是两⼤隐患
AI 叙事的脆弱性不仅体现在股票估值层⾯。
全球 AI 相关债务发⾏规模在 11 ⽉初已达 6870 亿美元，⽽超额流动性正在收缩，盈利上调动能也
在放缓。⾼盛估算， AI 投资约占美国实际 GDP 三季度同⽐增⻓的三分之⼆，⼀旦融资环境收

紧，负债最重的参与者将⾸当其冲，潜在冲击将远超科技⾏业范畴。
德意志银⾏策略师 George Saravelos 此前亦发出类似警告，指出外国持有的逾 20 万亿美元美国
股票中，约 90% 为账⾯价格升值⽽⾮实际买⼊，且整体对冲⽐率极低 —— 换⾔之，仅对冲⽐率
的结构性上移，就⾜以在⽆需⼤规模资本外流的情况下对美元形成实质性压制。
2027 年汇市格局：谁赢谁输
⾼盛 2027 年外汇总回报排名围绕四⼤主线展开：商品敏感货币随能源价格正常化⽽反弹、 AI 溢
出效应持续向亚洲货币传导、套息收益贡献收窄但依然为正、以及⽇元获得可持续政策⽀撑。
在预期总回报排名中，⾼息货币巴西雷亚尔（约 15% ）与南⾮兰特（约 10% ）位居前列；以⾊
列谢克尔、挪威克朗以及美元本⾝则处于末位。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking
the cash rate after the November increase. Since then, our
conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we
will publish the November Market Outlook with revised
forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing

market have all been revised down in 2027.
Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for
calendar 2027 more decisively inside the RBA’s 2–3% target
range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment
rate forecast has been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on
this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash rate from
here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.

A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The
housing market has undershot our forecasts over the past
couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been
coming in under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan
inquiries have taken another leg down in October after the
September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior
months, with the additional impact of the November rate hike
still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer.

Consumer income and spending have so far been quite
resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest
rates. But our Westpac Card Tracker is starting to show that
discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite
weak. We would not over-emphasise this point, but it does
imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB)

meeting, business survey data has shown weaker conditions
and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show
further evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-
labour cost shock that is not being fully passed through to
consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil
and fuel prices are already materially lower than the track
assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-
fourth lower than on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts

were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of
fuel costs to other prices, as well as easing consumer inflation
perceptions and expectations.
An RBA peak of greater conviction
Forecast changes
• A full update to our forecasts has been published in our
November Market Outlook.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
In the media conference following the November meeting,
Governor Bullock flagged that it was a good time to “take

stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the
market read this as a signal that the MPB would pause at the
December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to
downside risks than the internal members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is
looking even less likely given signs in the data and surveys.
The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and

senior officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a
downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though,
a rate hike in 2027 would require either a material new
inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would
constitute a policy error on the part of the RBA (something
that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the talents
and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with

the dissenting voters in the MPB that the downside risks need
more attention.
Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to
February, we think there will be more evidence that the
domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation
outlook with it.
“A deeper housing downturn and early signs of
cracks in the consumer sector point to an even
softer 2027."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
the labour market while showing significant concern over
the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the
passthrough of energy and technology input inflation. His
conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the monthly

rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward
supporting an increase in our policy rate of 25 basis points
at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI
inflation data out later today are similar to the past two
monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent
or even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also
kept the focus on inflation risks and stated or implied the FOMC

needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s
updates, pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January
meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by September. We believe
this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core
inflation ex shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-
month annualised basis and the labour market is balanced; but
it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate,

US households are under pressure from the cost of living, and
their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing
costs. Highlighting this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in
October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and
2010. Our November Market Outlook provide a full update on
our expectations for the US and the world.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index

fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest reading since
late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of
severe economic stress. Renewed cost-of-living and interest
rate pressures were front and centre following another lift
in inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the
RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage holders are feeling
the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group
falling to a record low. Consumers have become noticeably

more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the
relevant sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its
long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business
survey showed confidence slipped further to –15 in October as
conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic.
Of concern for the outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also
the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow
materially faster than selling prices, further compressing
already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter
financial conditions can jolt activity. The value of new dwelling
finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly
decline in the series’ history, taking approvals 25% lower year-
to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined

with the housing tax changes announced in May. Owner-
occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home
buyers proving particularly sensitive to the deterioration in
affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum
across the domestic economy, consistent with Westpac’s
expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside
activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our

expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the consensus
view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the
result, but core inflation was also marginally stronger than
forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the
past four months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look
to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost inflation through.
Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace,
gaining 0.3% in October.

The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated
by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor Waller prior to the
release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Post election, where are we left?
This week markets and the economy needed to digest
the General Election results, as well as data on how the
economy is tracking through the final months of the year. A
centre-right coalition looks more likely to ultimately prevail,

although it may be a while before we know for sure and
longer again before a stable government emerges. Watch
this space as paths to victory for the centre-left remain.
Economic data are consistent with a still-strong external
sector but a weaker domestically-generated growth
impulse. Our upcoming Economic Overview next week will
examine these trends in more depth.
The real star of the show this week was the outcome of the
2026 General Election, which delivered an election night lead

to the centre-right coalition of the National, ACT and NZ First
parties. The preliminary result was a 67-57 seat margin in an
enlarged 131 seat parliament. Hence the centre-right won on
election night with a slender one-seat margin, given that 66
seats are required to form a majority. Financial markets and
the business sector generally cheered this outcome given their
preference for a centre-right government. However, the margins
are tight and there is the potential for the result to switch around

when the final vote count becomes available on November 27.
National leads Labour by narrow margins in two electorates
(Wairarapa and Hamilton East) and could lose those seats
when all is said and done. Also, special votes tend to swing
more to the left than the right historically and hence could
boost the centre-left bloc. The star performer of the election
was the Opportunity Party which debuted in parliament with
7 seats. The centre-left would require their support to get

over the line in all scenarios, whereas the centre-right might or
might not need them depending on how the special votes and
final electorate results play out. There seems a clearer path to
victory for the centre-right – especially if they can come to an
agreement with Opportunity – but clients should be wary of
complacency (which we note may be a risk).
A further complication is that the election left the incumbent
National Party in a much-weakened position as their coalition

partners picked up seats at their expense, and because their
much-reduced share of the party vote (29.2%) meant that
no National Party list MPs made it into parliament. Several
seasoned and senior government ministers needed a list seat
to make it into the new government including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. Even if the final results confirm a centre-right
coalition win, there will be fierce debate within the coalition
on which party staffs key ministerial roles. ACT party leader

David Seymour is making a strong play for Finance Minister,
which Prime Minister Christopher Luxon is fiercely resisting.
There is the potential for tough and protracted coalition
negotiations to play out well into the Christmas period.
Indeed, I spoke on a post-election panel with an ex-National
Party senior minister this week who expressed the view that
it might not be until Waitangi Day (February 6) before a new
government is formed. Should anything like this play out then

the implications for economic and market uncertainty could
be significant. The initial relief rally in the New Zealand dollar
might prove premature in that scenario.
For interest rate markets, the main conclusion drawn is that as
a centre-left government seems a lower probability prospect,
then so also is the likelihood of a return to a ‘dual mandate’
for the RBNZ. Chief Economist Paul Conway made remarks
this week suggesting ongoing concern with inflation risks,

suggesting he is still in the hike camp at this stage. This has
seen markets firm up short-term OCR expectations such that a
25bp OCR increase in December is now seen as high probability
(85-90% currently). Bond markets cheered the prospect of
potentially tighter fiscal policy that could come with a centre-
right government. The spread between the 10-year government
bond and swap spread narrowed 4 basis points over the week
and the 10-year government bond yield differential to the US

fell to -49 basis points – the most negative since 2019.
Economic data showed further evidence of the two-speed
economy that is showing above-trend growth yet at the same
time feels quite unsatisfying to most voters. The low exchange
rate, generally robust global demand and tight supply
conditions in soft commodity markets have been delivering
consistently strong export returns for a couple of years now.
This week we saw another very strong GDT Pulse auction that

propelled whole milk powder futures higher – up some 20-25%
on year ago levels in USD terms. Significant upside risk for our
2026/27 Farmgate Milk Price forecast of $9.75/kgMS now exists,
which we will be delving into more deeply in our Economic
Overview to be released on Wednesday. The manufacturing
sector appears to remain in a solid state, with the manufacturing
PMI slipping a touch in October to 54.9 from 56.3 in September.
Short-term visitor arrivals have resumed their prior strong

uptrend, growing almost 3% for a second consecutive month
in September and almost 10% higher than a year earlier.
While arrivals from key European markets remain well below
year-earlier levels – likely reflecting the disruption to travel
through key Middle Eastern hubs along with higher jet fuel
prices – this has been more than offset by a very sharp lift in
arrivals from China. Indeed, Chinese arrivals in September
were almost two-thirds higher than the same month last year.

Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
And in the September quarter, arrivals from China surpassed
their pre-pandemic level for the first time. The number of
Australian citizens visiting New Zealand grew just under 6%yr
in September, but growth momentum from New Zealand’s
number one source market (a 30% share) does appear to have

slowed somewhat in recent months.
Less positive trends were evident in the more domestically
orientated areas of the economy. The most concrete indicator
of the NZ economy – concrete poured – dropped back a
touch in the September quarter, while consumer spending
in fell 0.7% in October. We have concerns on the quality
of the consumer spending data given that bank-sourced
data indicates a rise in consumer spending in October. But
nonetheless it’s probably reasonable to infer that higher fuel

costs in October crimped household budgets.
Housing market data for October were also week. House sales
fell and are down almost 22% y/y, while house prices fell in
October for the fifth consecutive month to be down almost
2% y/y. The weak performance of the housing market is likely
another factor weighing on consumer spending and residential
investment. That’s another topic we will be taking a look at in
next week’s Economic Overview.
Net migration flows for September confirm still modest

population growth. Arrivals of non-New Zealand citizens
continues to drift higher while departures declined a touch.
The net outflow of New Zealanders remains relatively high but
is declining, as signs of firmer employment growth emerge in
New Zealand at the same time as the Australian labour market
shows signs of weakening.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Nov 19, Last: –39.3, Westpac f/c: 30
Market f/c: 27.5, Range: 9.9 to 42
Employment fell by 39.3k in September, well below expectations
for a 20k lift. Some of this weakness may reflect an unwind of
a possible residual seasonality stemming from recent changes

to the survey design, which saw August come in stronger.
Under the monthly noise, the labour market is loosening
gradually as jobs growth runs slower than population growth.
While some leading indicators point to near-term stability
– such as job ads – business surveys and detail within the
employment data point to some downside risk.
For October, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of +30k,
which would see employment growth hold steady on a three-

month average basis.
AUS: Nov Westpac Housing Sentiment (index)
Nov 17, Last: 85.3
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index that appears in our
regular Housing Pulse reports will now be published on a
monthly basis a week after Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment is released. The Index is a composite
based on housing-related sentiment measures from the survey
and has proven to be an extremely useful predictor of turning
points in housing markets, both nationally and across states.

At 85.3, the Oct monthly Index read was holding near historic
lows, well below the long run avg of 100. The Nov update will
capture reactions to the RBA’s latest interest rate rise and the
deepening and broadening housing downturn. The release
will also include results from two new questions added to
the regular survey on consumers’ property buying and selling
intentions over the next twelve months.
AUS: Q3 Wage Price Index (%qtr)
Nov 18, Last: 0.8, Westpac f/c: 1.0

Market f/c: 1.0, Range: 0.9 to 1.1
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 0.8% in Q2, leaving annual
growth at 3.2%. Private sector wages continued to decelerate,
in line with a softer labour market, while public sector wages
provided a bit of an offset.
The Fair Work Commission's (FWC) 2026 Annual Wage Review
handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award
wages. This will have a noticeable impact on the Q3 WPI, as the
wage increases stemming from the review are typically passed

on during the September quarter.
We have pencilled in a rise of 1.0% for Q3, lifting the annual
pace to 3.4%yr. Detail on wages growth by bargaining stream
will be closely watched for evidence of broader diffusion
beyond arrangements directly linked to the FWC's decision.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Nov 19, Last: 4.7, Westpac f/c: 4.7
Market f/c: 4.7, Range: 4.6 to 4.8
Once again, the surprise in employment coincided with a surprise
in labour force participation, which fell 0.2ppts to 66.8% from
its near cycle high of 67.0% in August. This translated to a 26.2k

decline in the size of the labour force, which was less than the
drop in employment. As a result, the unemployment rate ticked
slightly higher in September, from 4.65% to 4.72% (although it
was steady at 4.7% to one decimal place), a new cycle high.
For October, we have the participation rate steady at 66.8%,
which together with the forecast rise in employment, would
see the unemployment rate also hold steady at 4.7%. Early
signs of rising retrenchments are a key signal to watch over the

coming months, especially if new hiring across other areas of
the economy starts to dry up.
NZ: Oct Selected Consumer Prices
Nov 17
Stats NZ’s monthly Selected Prices provides an update on
around half of the full CPI, including many of the more volatile
items. In terms of the big groups, we’re expecting a 0.1%
rise in overall food prices, with fresh produce prices typically
soft at this time of year. We also expect continued softness
in housing rents. However, the key focus will be on transport

costs. Petrol prices are expected to be up 11%. And we’ll be
watching to see if high global fuel prices are contributing to
further increases in airfares after recent large increases. There
is related uncertainty around holiday accommodation costs
(which drive much of the month-to-month volatility in overall
prices).
NZ: Q4 RBNZ Survey of Expectations (%yr)
Nov 17, Inflation 2yrs Ahead, Last: 2.34
With an easing in headline inflation in the September

quarter, we’re likely to see some easing in near-term inflation
expectations (one year ahead). However, longer term inflation
expectations, particularly the closely-watched two years
ahead measure, are likely to remain broadly steady. While
interest rates are moving higher, businesses are continuing to
report pressures on operating costs. In addition, despite their
recent easing, oil prices remain elevated. The only time longer-
term inflation expectations have moved materially below 2.3%

was in the mid-2010s. That followed an extended period when
inflation was running below 2%.

Document 339: Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China
Date:    8 hours ago
Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged
Chips & Deflating China
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 16 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Tonik / Unsplash

I. Cyber Glitches & Chip Controls
"Once is happenstance. Twice is coincidence. Three times is enemy
action." That's according to Ian Fleming’s Goldfinger (1959)

### Page 2

What, then, should we make of the recent string of technology
disruptions? In October, hackers tampered with freezers at roughly 700
French supermarkets, spoiling food and triggering fires. Last Thursday,
Microsoft suffered a 10-hour outage affecting Outlook and Teams. On
Friday, the Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) imposed the longest
nationwide flight ground stop since 9/11. Then, today, customers at major
US banks, including JPMorgan Chase, reported problems accessing their

accounts, while a computer failure temporarily grounded United Airlines
flights.
France blamed Iran's Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps for the
supermarket attacks, although Tehran denies responsibility. The other
disruptions appear to have been technical failures, including software
bugs at Microsoft and JPMorgan Chase and a malfunctioning router at the
FAA. United's outage was resolved within 90 minutes. No evidence links
these incidents, but their timing is curious, to say the least.

Regulatory loopholes might also be considered glitches, and one may
finally be getting fixed. On Monday, the US Commerce Department
announced tighter restrictions to prevent Chinese companies from
accessing American AI chips and computing power through overseas data
centers, particularly in Southeast Asia. The measures follow scrutiny of
Nvidia's ties to Singapore-based Megaspeed, which has been accused of
circumventing US export controls.
The announcement added to the semiconductor stock selloff as investors

worried about lost chip sales and slower overseas data center investment.
The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index (SOX) plunged 5.3% today, while
the iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) fell 5.4%. Nvidia declined 4.1%,
AMD 5.6%, and Micron more than 7%. The Nasdaq Composite dropped
2.5%, remaining below its 200-day moving average (charts).

### Page 3

Investors are particularly worried about Nvidia. How much of its revenue
could tighter export controls put at risk? Will CEO Jensen Huang's
departure unravel Nvidia's circular financing strategy and the broader AI
investment boom? Tomorrow's earnings call, led by interim CEO Colette
Kress, could provide crucial answers.
In any event, money appears to be rotating out of technology and into the
broader market. The S&P 500 equal-weight stock index has outperformed

its market-weight counterpart in recent weeks (chart). The former remains
near its recent record high, while the latter is down 5.1% from its record
high on October 5. The Nasdaq is 11.2% below its October 5 record high.
SOXX is down 35.3% from its June 22 record high. The market is holding
up remarkably well given the shellacking of the semiconductor stocks.

### Page 4

II. China Is Weakening
China's economy continues to lose steam, with domestic demand
particularly weak. Consumers are depressed. Deflation is mounting
because of excess production. Here’s more:
(1) Retail sales & production. Retail sales rose just 0.4% y/y in October.
Inflation-adjusted retail sales fell 0.4%, highlighting persistent weakness in
domestic demand. Both measures are hovering around zero, a sharp
contrast to the double-digit growth rates that prevailed before the

pandemic.
Chinese consumers remain deeply pessimistic, with the consumer
confidence index far below its historical average (chart). They've been hit

### Page 5

by a huge negative wealth effect in the property market. They've also
been losing money in the stock and gold markets this year.
Industrial production rose 4.8% y/y in October despite declining real retail
sales, highlighting the imbalance between China's manufacturing output
and domestic consumption that pushes Chinese producers to dump more
goods onto global markets (chart).
Automobile production remains near record highs at an annualized 27.9
million vehicles, despite weak domestic demand (chart). Total domestic

passenger vehicle retail sales over the trailing 12-month period through
October came to roughly 19.5 million units, down about 20% y/y. The glut
has been exported.

### Page 6

(2) Inflation. China's inflation data further confirm weak domestic demand
and excess production. Headline CPI rose just 0.7% y/y in October, while
prices excluding food and energy declined 0.4% y/y (chart).
The prolonged property downturn is a major drag on Chinese consumers.
New house prices across 70 cities fell 2.4% y/y in October (chart). New
house prices have been falling since 2022.

### Page 7

Financial markets reflect these challenges. Chinese real estate stocks
remain deeply depressed, with the Shenzhen Real Estate Index near
levels last seen during the global financial crisis (chart). The CSI 300 has
been volatile and trendless for years.
(3) Trade. China's manufacturing overcapacity is increasingly spilling into
global markets. Merchandise exports have surged to a record $4.2 trillion
annualized, far outpacing imports of $3.1 trillion (chart).

### Page 8

As a result, China's trade surplus reached a record $1.27 trillion over the
past 12 months (chart).
China's crude oil imports, adjusted for Brent prices, are flattening after
decades of growth (chart). That's largely because more Chinese are
driving electric vehicles. This development was weighing on global oil
prices until the war in the Middle East boosted them this year.

### Page 9

China's exporters are increasingly looking beyond the US, with shipments
to Europe surging to nearly $890 billion over the past year, almost twice
the $463 billion shipped to America (chart).
Exports to Vietnam have also soared, to a record $250 billion, alongside
strong growth in shipments to South Korea and India (chart).

### Page 10

(4) Money & credit. China's credit boom has left its banking system with
enormous outstanding loans of $41.9 trillion, almost three times the $14.2
trillion outstanding at US banks (chart).
Bank loan growth continues to slow, with bank loans and M2 growth rates
in clear downward trends (chart).

### Page 11

Weak bank credit growth reflects the broader economic malaise, even as
Chinese interest rates remain exceptionally low (chart).
The People's Bank of China continues accumulating gold, reflecting its
efforts to diversify official reserves (chart).

### Page 12

clause.

Document 419: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 18
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

Document 450: US SECTORS CALL_ Financials, Health Care & Consumer Staples

Source PDF: all report pdf/US SECTORS CALL_ Financials, Health Care & Consumer Staples.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US SECTORS CALL: Financials, Health Care & Consumer Staples
Date:    5 hours ago
US SECTORS CALL: Financials, Health Care & Consumer Staples

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US SECTORS CALL: Financials,
Health Care & Consumer
Staples
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 15 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Stocks bounced back last week after the prior week’s AI selloff. The S&P
500 rose 2.5% and is up 10.5% ytd. The chip trade rebounded, but
leadership changed hands. Nvidia’s board opened an investigation into
allegations against CEO Jensen Huang, who resigned on Thursday. CFO
Colette Kress is interim CEO. Investors moved into the stocks of Nvidia’s
rivals. AMD rose 19% for the week, and Broadcom rose 18% as Nvidia fell
13%.
Health Care and Financials have outperformed since the market’s March

30 low. Health Care is up 31.1% and Financials 21.1%, both ahead of the
S&P 500’s 19.2% (chart). Staples has lagged, up just 12.3% over the
same period, even though it's up 20.2% ytd.
Here’s more on Financials, Health Care, and Consumer Staples:
(1) Financials. The IPO window is wide open. SpaceX raised $75 billion
in June, the largest IPO ever, and its shares rose 19% on the first day.
Anthropic’s shares are expected to begin trading on Thursday at $70-$78

each, implying a fully diluted valuation of $1.26 trillion to $1.40 trillion. Its
S-1 filing affirmed that AI customers will pay up for compute. In Q2,
Anthropic earned $2.08 of revenue per dollar of compute and
infrastructure expense, up fivefold from $0.40 in Q1-2025 (in Q3, however,
the figure dipped to $1.72, as the company bought costlier short-term
capacity to meet demand). Big AI listings bring fees for underwriters,
trading desks, and the asset managers who buy the deals (chart).

### Page 3

Analysts have expected a strong year for capital markets. They
collectively forecast Investment Banking & Brokerage revenues to grow
21.1% this year, up from 7.4% in December (chart). Earnings are forecast
to grow 30.9%. Yet the industry’s forward P/E has fallen to 13.6 from 19.1
earlier this year. Investors are not paying for the deal boom.
The Financials sector’s forward profit margin is a record 22.1%, and the
profitability is broad-based. Forward profit margins are at or near record

highs across the sector’s biggest industries: Asset Management &
Custody Banks (28.6%), Regional Banks (28.5%), Investment Banking
(28.1%), and Diversified Banks (27.8%) (chart).

### Page 4

The Financials sector’s forward P/E has fallen to 15.2 from 16.4 at year-
end 2025, while its forward earnings is up 14.0% ytd. The sector has been
getting cheaper as it gets more profitable. We retain our overweight rating
on Financials.
(2) Health Care. Health Care has been the best-performing sector since
March 30. It is up 21.8% ytd. Forward earnings is up 11.5% ytd, and the
forward P/E is up 9.4% (chart). It trades at 19.8 times forward earnings, a

premium to the S&P 500’s 18.2.
Health Care’s profitability has been turning up. The sector’s forward profit
margin has risen to 8.7% from a low of 7.9% in January (chart). Within the
sector, the Biotechnology industry’s forward profit margin is at 33.4% and
Pharmaceuticals’ is at 31.1%; both have been climbing.

### Page 5

The Pharmaceuticals industry accounts for 37% of the sector’s market
capitalization. Analysts collectively expect Pharma earnings to fall 5.9%
this year, then jump 29.6% in 2027 (chart). But they’ve more than doubled
their revenue sights this year: The consensus 2026 revenue estimate
implies growth of 9.9%, up from 4.0% in December. The rebound rests on
sales, not just a low base from which the growth is measured.
AI should speed progress up in the medical world. In Anthropic’s IPO

roadshow video on Tuesday, CEO Dario Amodei claimed that AI can
compress 50 to 100 years of medical research into five to ten. We think
the benefits to drug discovery could be huge.
Investors already see it. Moderna is up 623.8% ytd following the success
of its personalized cancer vaccine, designed with the help of machine

### Page 6

learning. Clinical trials and approvals still take years, so the payoff will
build over time. Pharma’s 2027 rebound doesn’t depend on it. We
continue to recommend overweighting Health Care.
(3) Consumer Staples. Staples is one of only three sectors that got more
expensive this year. Its forward P/E has risen to 23.9 from 20.8 at the end
of 2025. Over the same period, the S&P 500’s P/E fell to 18.2 from 22.2,
and IT’s fell to 17.2 from 26.5 (chart). Only Real Estate and Consumer

Discretionary trade at higher multiples.
Forward earnings is up just 4.6% ytd, while the forward P/E is up 13.5%
(chart). Almost all of that re-rating came after late September, when
investors fled the AI trade for defensive stocks.

### Page 7

Profitability has not improved. The sector’s forward profit margin is 7.1%,
below its 2021 peak of 7.7% (chart). Analysts have cut their 2027 earnings
growth estimate to 5.6% from 7.3% in August. Investors are paying 23.9
times forward earnings for single-digit-percentage growth.
Walmart and Target are set to report quarterly earnings next week. The
Merchandise Retail industry trades at 36.4 times forward earnings, its
highest forward P/E since earlier this year (chart). Investors pay up for

Consumer Staples stocks when they fear an economic slowdown. But the
labor market is picking up, and consumers are spending. That makes the
valuation premium characteristic of defensive sectors hard to justify. We
retain our underweight rating on Consumer Staples.

### Page 8

clause.

Document 532: 世界的なインフレ環境と｢異空間｣にある中国の物価動向 ～企業部門ではインフレも、家計部門はディスインフレが続き、金融政策_4eb5b875

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 11 月 9 日

1/3 
世界的なインフレ環境と｢異空間｣にある中国の物価動向 
～企業部門ではインフレも、家計部門はディスインフレが続き、金融政策の自由度も低下～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ イラン情勢の悪化に伴うエネルギー価格の上昇を受け、世界的にインフレが加速し、金融引
き締めの動きが広がっている。中国でも原材料価格の上昇を背景に、10 月の生産者物価は購
入価格、出荷価格ともに2022 年６月以来の高い伸びとなり、企業部門ではコスト上昇圧力が
強まっている。一方、消費者物価は生鮮品を中心とする食料品価格の下落や内需の弱さを背
景に伸びが鈍化しており、企業部門がインフレ、家計部門がディスインフレに直面する二極
化が続いている。こうした状況は企業収益を圧迫するとともに、不動産市場を巡る懸念も重
なり、株式相場の重しとなっている。中国当局は株式市場の下支え策を打ち出しているもの
の、市場マインドの改善には力不足とみられる。世界的な金融引き締めが進むなか、資金流
出や人民元安への懸念から大幅な金融緩和にも踏み切りにくく、中国当局は景気下支えと金
融市場の安定の両立に向けて難しい政策運営を迫られている。

イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油をはじめとする商品市況の上昇は、世界的なエネルギー価格の上
昇を招いている。さらに、エネルギー価格の上昇は幅広い経済活動においてコスト上昇を引き起こ
しており、多くの国でインフレが加速する動きがみられる。その結果、米国やユーロ圏、日本をは
じめとする先進国のみならず、新興国においても中央銀行が金融引き締めに動く流れが広がりをみ
せている。このように、世界的に金融政策を取り巻く環境は大きく変化している。 
中国においても、企業部門はエネルギーインフレの影響を免れない状況にある。川上段階の物価
にあたる 10 月の生産者物価（購入価格）は、前年同月比＋7.9％と前月（同＋6.9％）から加速し
て、2022 年６月以来の高い伸びとなった（図１）。前月比は＋1.1％と前月（同＋1.2％）からわず
かに上昇ペースは鈍化したものの、３ヵ月連続で上昇するなど、物価上昇圧力に直面している。原
油・天然ガスなどエネルギー関連で物価上昇圧力が強まるとともに、原材料価格の上昇を反映して

### Page 2

2/3 
化学工業関連などで上昇基調が続いて
いることが影響している。こうした原材
料価格の上昇を反映して、生産者物価
（出荷価格）も前年同月比＋5.7％と前
月（同＋5.0％）から加速して、2022 年
６月以来の高い伸びとなった。前月比は
＋0.7％と前月（同＋1.2％）からペース
は鈍化したものの、３ヵ月連続で上昇し
ており、中間財を中心に価格上昇の動き
が確認されるなど、企業間取引段階では
価格転嫁の動きが広がっている。ただ
し、生活資料価格は前月比で下落しており、耐久消費財価格も下落するなど、最終消費財への価格
転嫁のハードルの高さがうかがえる。 
一方で、足元ではエルニーニョ現象の発生を受けて、世界的に異常気象の動きがみられるなか、
イラン情勢の悪化による肥料価格の高止まりも重なり、農業生産の低迷が食料インフレを引き起こ
す動きがみられる。このため、家計部門にとっては食料品とエネルギーという生活必需品の物価上
昇が懸念される。ただし、10 月の消費者
物価は前年同月比＋0.7％となり、前月
（同＋0.9％）から鈍化して引き続き政
府目標（２％前後）を下回る伸びにとど
まる（図２）。前月比も＋0.0％と前月（同
＋0.2％）から上昇ペースが鈍化してお
り、インフレ圧力が後退するなど家計部
門を取り巻く環境は異なる。豚肉（前月
比▲1.0％）や野菜（同▲5.8％）、卵（同
▲5.2％）、海産物（同▲1.3％）、果物（同
▲0.6％）など生鮮品を中心とする食料
品価格が軒並み下落したことが影響した。食料品とエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率は前年同月
比＋0.8％と前月（同＋1.0％）から鈍化した。前月比も＋0.0％と前月（同＋0.0％）から２ヵ月連
続の横ばいで推移しており、前述の通り企業部門が製品価格にコスト上昇を転嫁できないことに加
え、雇用環境が厳しさを増すなかでサービス物価も上昇圧力に乏しい。

りません。 
3/3 
足元の中国では、企業部門がインフレに、家計部門がディスインフレに直面する『異世界』が続
いている。企業部門にとって、コスト上昇を製品価格に転嫁することが難しい状況は、収益の圧迫
につながっている。そのうえ、不動産開発企業を巡る事業環境の悪化や債務問題の再燃に対する懸
念も重なり、関連株を中心に下押し圧力
がかかるなど、株式相場の重しとなって
いる（図３）。こうしたなか、中国人民銀
行（中銀）と国家金融監督管理総局、中
国証券監督管理委員会は２日、証券・基
金・保険会社向けのスワップ制度の拡充
や、株式買い戻し・買い増し向け再貸出
金利の引き下げなど、株式相場の下支え
に向けた措置を打ち出した。さらに、中
国証券監督管理委員会が投資銀行に対
して新規株式公開（ＩＰＯ）の一時停止
を求めるとともに、既存株主による大口の株式売却を制限したとも報じられた。しかし、こうした
対応は既存の政策枠組みの拡充が中心であり、冷え込んだ市場マインドを好転させるには力不足と
受け止められている模様である。世界的に金融引き締めの動きが広がりをみせるなか、中国当局に
とっては資金流出や人民元安への懸念から大幅な金融緩和に動くことも難しく、景気下支えと金融
市場の安定の両立に向けて難しい政策の舵取りを迫られる局面が続くことになろう。

2026 年 10 月 30 日 全 3 頁

中国：綱紀粛正強化と経済の萎縮 
中央委員は5 年で 2 割減の異常。問われることのない任命責任

調査本部 主席研究員 齋藤 尚登

[要約] 
 中国共産党第 20 期中央委員会第 5 回全体会議（五中全会）が、2026 年 10 月 26 日から
29 日にかけて北京で開催され、「中国共産党が全面的かつ厳格な統治を持続的に推進
するためのいくつかの重大問題に関する決定」を採択した。中国では汚職・腐敗撲滅、
綱紀粛正の嵐が吹いている。 
 今後、体制の引き締めが維持・強化される見通しであり、経済活動が一部萎縮するリ
スクに注意する必要がある。汚職・腐敗撲滅や綱紀粛正が強化される中、習近平総書
記への忠誠を高めることが最も重要なポイントとなる。この点で、習近平氏の関心が
どこに向かうかが決定的に重要だが、関心は経済よりも政治に向かいやすい。「景気テ
コ入れ」と「綱紀粛正」の指示が同時に出れば、「綱紀粛正」の影響が勝ることになろ
う。こうした状況下では、悪目立ちをしないでやり過ごす、「不作為」という状況に陥
りやすくなる。大和総研では、2027 年の成長率見通しを前年比 4.4％と、2026 年の同
4.6％から若干減速するとみているのは、この影響を懸念しているからだ。

### Page 2

1

2 / 3

汚職・腐敗撲滅、綱紀粛正の嵐 
中国共産党第 20 期中央委員会第 5 回全体会議（五中全会）が、2026 年 10 月 26 日から 29 日
にかけて北京で開催され、「中国共産党が全面的かつ厳格な統治を持続的に推進するためのい
くつかの重大問題に関する決定」（以下、決定）を採択して閉幕した。決定の中で印象的だっ
たのは「不敢腐、不能腐、不想腐」（腐敗させず、腐敗できず、腐敗したいと思わない）とい
うフレーズだ。腐敗・汚職撲滅と綱紀粛正の継続・強化が随所で言及されている。 
中国では汚職・腐敗撲滅、綱紀粛正の嵐が吹いている。五中全会に参加した中央委員は 162
名と発表された。2022 年 10 月に習近平政権 3 期目がスタートした当時は 205 名であり、それ
との比較では 21.0％の減少だ。ただし、この間に中央委員会候補委員から中央委員に昇格した
人々1がいることを考えると、減少の延べ人数はもっと多いことになる。全体の 2 割だとしても
異常な多さであり、汚職・腐敗撲滅、綱紀粛正の凄まじさを物語っている。こうした状況下で
習近平氏の任命責任が厳しく問われてもおかしくないが、そのような声は全く聞かれない。 
今回の五中全会では、馬興瑞・元政治局委員、張又侠・元政治局委員（元党中央軍事委員会
副主席）ら 33 名の党籍剥奪処分を改めて確認した。うち 16 名が軍部であった。党中央軍事委
員会は習近平政権の 3 期目発足時点では 7 名体制でスタートしたが、失脚に次ぐ失脚で、2026
年 1 月以降は習近平主席と張昇民副主席の 2 名体制となっていた（図表 1）。五中全会では、張
昇民副主席の政治局委員への昇格が発表されたが、党軍事委員会の補充は行われなかった。指
揮系統の中枢で 5 名分の空席が補充されない異常事態が続いている。

図表1 党中央軍事委員会メンバー（2022 年 10 月の発足当時からの変化）

「不作為」による経済の萎縮リスク 
五中全会では、中国共産党第 21 回全国代表大会を 2027 年下半期に北京で開催することが発
表された（通常は、10 月か 11 月に開催される）。これは 5 年に一度開催される最重要会議であ
り、直後の第 21 期中央委員会第 1 回全体会議（一中全会）で今後 5 年間の新指導体制が決定さ
れる。現状では、習近平総書記の 4 期目突入がほぼ確実視され、その後継候補となり得る 1970
